Phase `environment` stays NOT_REACHED. Is that \"honest\"? The runtime prerequisite (engine attestation) is arguably the environment phase. Per manifest item 3: \"至少有阶段 identity、mainRepository、scratchRepository、environment、paths、wer、protectedHashes；未进入阶段必须显式为 NOT_REACHED，当前失败阶段标 FAILED\". If the engine precondition fails, that's a failure *in* the environment/runtime prerequisite stage — arguably `environment` should be FAILED, and its attestation should be recorded. But the phase `environment` remains NOT_REACHED even though the script actually collected engine attestation. Hmm, that's a potential P2 finding: the runtime prerequisite failure (the very failure mode that killed R1F) is not marked as a phase failure; phases all remain NOT_REACHED, and stopCategory=RUNTIME_PREREQUISITE. Is that inconsistent with \"当前失败阶段标 FAILED\"? The phases list doesn't include a \"runtimePrerequisite\" phase, so the failure has no phase. Since $Result.process contains the attestation, one could argue the environment phase should be IN_PROGRESS/FAILED. This is a genuine traceability gap: post-run adjudication reading `phases` would see all NOT_REACHED and cannot tell which phase failed from phases alone (though stopCategory does). Severity: P2 (documentation/state-model consistency), not P1 since stopCategory+stopReason+process attestation do capture it.\n\nWait, but check: does the environment phase later include engine attestation? Let me continue reading. The $Result.process is set earlier (line 615), separate from $Result.environment. Let's see what environment phase does.\n\n2. Line 612-613: Get-Command with -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue; then availability checked; fail-stop if null. OK.\n\n3. Note the candidate script itself does NOT re-verify the module attestation (Utility/Security module name/version/path); the manifest says the *preflight* (Codex-run) records that. The candidate asserts command availability only. The dispatch prompt says \"Add explicit reached/not-reached phase reporting and truthful engine attestation for the fixed PowerShell 7.6.6 executable\". Manifest item 3 says candidate records child runtime. So candidate only needs runtime evidence. But Task AC2 in the review prompt asks: \"固定 PowerShell 7.6.6 executable、edition、PSHOME、language mode、Get-FileHash/Get-Acl 命令与模块 attestation/precondition 是否精确、可审计\". In the candidate, psHome is recorded but NOT compared to expected 'C:\\Program Files\\PowerShell\\7'. The `process` block records expectedExecutablePath/version/edition/languageMode and *MatchesExpected flags, but no expectedPsHome/psHomeMatchesExpected. The manifest's preflight PASS requires psHome=C:\\Program Files\\PowerShell\\7; the candidate records psHome but doesn't assert it. Also no module attestation (Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility/Security name/version/path) in the candidate. Is that required for the candidate? Task AC2: \"增加显式 phase reached/not-reached 状态与精确 engine attestation\" — PSModulePath/module attestation is a preflight requirement (AC5, Codex). The candidate's engine attestation \"记录 `executablePath`、`powerShellVersion`、`psEdition`、`psHome`、`languageMode`，以及 `Get-FileHash` 和 `Get-Acl` 的存在性、来源与版本，并做运行时前置条件断言，不匹配立即 fail-stop\" per handoff line 39. Also Get-FileHash/Get-Acl *source* is recorded but not checked (e.g., that the source is from the expected PowerShell module family). Manifest line 42 says module names/version/path recorded as evidence, not allowlist; \"both required commands resolve from the expected PowerShell module family\" is a PASS requirement for the preflight (parent), not necessarily child. So for the candidate, the gap is: psHome mismatch does not fail-stop → a candidate running under e.g. a shim with PS 7.6.6 but different PSHOME would pass its own attestation. Practically, executable path match implies psHome. Severity: P3 or P2. Hmm.\n\nActually wait — is executablePath checked via MainModule.FileName with OrdinalIgnoreCase equals the fixed path? Yes line 628. That's a strong check. psHome derived. So the omission of psHome assertion is minor → P3.\n\nAlso the failure of `$currentProcess.MainModule.FileName` may throw for some processes; if it throws with $ErrorActionPreference='Stop', the catch-all at the end handles it (need to see the outer try/catch). Let's continue.\n\n4. Line 15: evidence target Test-Path check then throw before anything. Good; but note the throw happens before constructing $Result; the catch-all presumably writes partial evidence... wait, if the target already exists, we throw BEFORE $Result exists, so nothing written — good (protect scene, no overwrite). Need to check catch block handles $Result being null → but the throw at line 15 occurs before try block at 603, so it propagates as an unhandled script error → script exits with error. It's a P3 (new field value unreachable in practice) — \"新增 stopReason 'PROTECTED_HASH_FAILED' 在无 return/throw 的情况下被后续聚合分类覆盖，实际不可观测\".\n\nHmm wait, is it fully overwritten? Line 1040's if-condition includes invalidHashCount -gt 0. If invalidHashCount > 0 then yes, condition true → overwritten. And 931 only triggers when invalidHashCount > 0. So yes, always overwritten. Unless... line 1040 is inside the same block and `$Result.stopCategory`/`stopReason` are set unconditionally there (line 1041-1043). So 'PROTECTED_HASH_FAILED' is unreachable in the final evidence. Definitive: P3 (dead classification) — or is it P2 because the manifest requires \"错误要区分 runtime prerequisite、repository prehash、inventory/query stop\" and this one is an inventory/query stop anyway, so the classification contract is still met. P3. But the impact on adjudication: the reviewer of evidence would see phases.protectedHashes=FAILED and stopReason=...HASH_INCOMPLETE, which is fine. OK, P3.\n\n- R1F's catch block (875-878) had no phase flip; R1G adds the IN_PROGRESS→FAILED flip ✓ (good).\n\n- R1F's serialization-failure path had no stopCategory; R1G adds stopCategory='INVENTORY_OR_QUERY_STOP' (line 1063) with stopReason='EVIDENCE_SERIALIZATION_FAILED'. Hmm — R1G's new stopCategory assignment for a serialization failure is 'INVENTORY_OR_QUERY_STOP', which is semantically wrong (it's an evidence-serialization failure, not an inventory/query stop). Similarly size-limit path sets stopCategory='INVENTORY_OR_QUERY_STOP' (1071) for EVIDENCE_SIZE_LIMIT_PARTIAL. This *contradicts* the handoff's claim (line 41) that stopCategory distinguishes EVIDENCE_SERIALIZATION_FAILED / EVIDENCE_SIZE_LIMIT_PARTIAL / EVIDENCE_WRITE_FAILED. So the new stopCategory feature actively misclassifies those two non-query failures as query stops. Impact: an adjudicator filtering on stopCategory would classify an evidence-write/serialization failure as an inventory/query stop, potentially mis-blaming the host filesystem query rather than the evidence pipeline. That's a real (if modest) defect in the new classification feature → P2.\n\nI think P2 is right: it's a correctness issue in the newly added classification surface that the Handoff explicitly documents incorrectly.\n\n- Also note EVIDENCE_WRITE_FAILED: on write failure the script throws $writeError (line 1092), no stopCategory assignment at all. So the evidence file may not exist at all in that case (throw before writing... well the write partially failed). Since nothing is written, no stopCategory is observable. It's just the stderr/exit. Honest? The handoff claims stopCategory distinguishes it. Again a doc/code mismatch. Same finding.\n\nNow, next: does the R1G evidence emit `phases` even in the partial-evidence path? Yes New-PartialEvidence includes phases ✓.\n\nNow, the R1F vs R1G `command` field: R1F line 24 says 'Windows PowerShell -NoLogo ...'; R1G line 24 says 'pwsh.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File verification/P02/p02pre-r1g-capture.ps1' ✓ matches the manifest's exact capture argv ✓.\n\nNote: manifest says the capture child argv = `-NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File verification/P02/p02pre-r1g-capture.ps1` with CWD=G:\\CODEX\\New Manga ✓ — the script's self-recorded `command` string matches, though the script cannot verify the actual argv it was launched with (it records a constant). Is that a problem? The `cwd` is recorded from Get-Location ✓; the argv is a *claim*, not verified. R1F had the same (constant string). The parent runner records the actual argv. So a reader shouldn't treat the evidence's `command` field as proof. Minor: the evidence JSON field `command` is a constant literal, which could be misread as attested argv. R1F-inherited. I might mention as P3 or in residual risk. Since the manifest explicitly requires the *parent runner* to record actual executable/argv/CWD/PID and warns not to over-claim (line 29), and the candidate's `process` block records `executablePath` from MainModule (actual) — note the candidate doesn't record its own actual argv. Hmm, `$Result.process` has no `commandLine`. Would be nice. Not a defect per AC. Mention as P3? I'd rather fold into residual risk/notes to avoid noise. Actually the review prompt item 2 asks whether engine attestation is \"精确、可审计\" — I can note that the `command` literal is unverified and that `process` lacks `psHomeMatchesExpected`/module attestation; combine into one P3 finding about attestation precision.\n\nWait, one more important item for item 2: the manifest's preflight requires `languageMode=FullLanguage`. The candidate asserts FullLanguage ✓. But hold on — the candidate is launched by the parent with `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -File`. LanguageMode under a normal user is FullLanguage unless WDAC/AppLocker forces ConstrainedLanguage. Fine.\n\nNow, item 4 of my mandate: \"路径规范化、repository boundary、staging 子树排除是否在所有 filesystem getter/hash/ACL 前生效\". In Get-RepositoryRecord the order is: Get-PathChain(root), Get-PathChain(.git), Get-Item root/.git, Get-FileRecord(.git/index) [prehash], then git commands, then for each Git path: Test-SafeRepositoryRelativePath → full path boundary check → Test-UnreadUserDataStagingPath → then Get-Item component loop → Get-FileHash. ✓ exclusion before getters. ✓\n\nBut note: `$record.gitMetadataBefore = Get-FileRecord (Join-Path $Root '.git\\index')` happens BEFORE git commands and before path normalization of inventory. Fine (not a candidate path).\n\nOne more: in the loop, `$full` is computed at line 412 and used for Get-FileHash at 448; but the component-walk uses `$cursor = Join-Path $cursor $part` with raw `$parts` from `$relative` — if `$relative` contains e.g. a trailing space component (rejected by Test-SafeRepositoryRelativePath) or `..` (rejected) ✓. Since each part is validated by Test-SafeRepositoryRelativePath (rejects '..', ':', trailing dot/space, '~[0-9]'), OK.\n\nBut subtle: the component walk uses `$relative` while the boundary check uses `$full` derived from `$normalizedRelative`. If `$relative` differs from `$normalizedRelative` only by case (allowed), then `$cursor` (built from raw) and `$full` (built from normalized, with '/'→'\\') could differ in case; Get-FileHash uses `$full` (from normalized) while Get-Item chain uses raw case. On Windows, case-insensitive → same file. ✓ R1F-inherited anyway.\n\nPotential bypass: `Test-SafeRepositoryRelativePath` rejects parts matching `~[0-9]` anywhere — good for 8.3. ADS rejected by ':'. What about paths with Unicode normalization tricks or a part like `...`? `$part -in @('.','..')` only catches exact; `...` is a legal Windows filename. Fine.\n\nWhat about a relative path like `.tmp.driveupload.` (trailing dot) → Test-Safe rejects (EndsWith('.')) ✓ before any access.\n\nWhat about a path enumerated by git that is `foo/../.tmp.driveupload/x`? '..' rejected ✓.\n\nHmm, what about the exclusion check using OrdinalIgnoreCase prefix `.tmp.driveupload/` — for a path like `.TMP.DRIVEUPLOAD/x` git wouldn't produce it normally but if it did, excluded ✓ (self-check line 167).\n\nWhat about a *nested* `.tmp.driveupload` deeper, e.g. `sub/.tmp.driveupload/x`? Test-UnreadUserDataStagingPath only matches a leading `.tmp.driveupload`, so a nested one would be read/hashed. But it increases the diff size — \"minimal difference\" claim (manifest line 30: \"除上述状态字段/engine attestation 与 runtime contract 外，代码行为应保持最小差异\") — restructuring control flow is arguably a behavior-preserving necessity for phase tracking. Let me evaluate its \"可疑点\" critically:\n\nC1 ✓ (my P3).\nC2 same as C1.\nC3 — the last block continues to ACL rechecks even after the protectedHashes phase fails. Wait, careful: is that true? Line 931-936 sets phases.protectedHashes='FAILED' and stopReason, but no return; then line 941+ runs the ACL comparisons. So after a protected-hash failure, the script continues doing more filesystem queries. That's a *divergence from fail-stop semantics* — \"错误立即停止\" is claimed in the Handoff (item 1: 继承所有失败即停语义). But R1F had the same structure (R1F line 826-830 equivalent? Let me verify: R1F's protected-hash failure — did R1F set stopReason and continue?). R1F grep showed no 'PROTECTED_HASH_FAILED' in R1F; in R1F the protectedHashes loop failure... let me check R1F lines ~820-835. In R1F, was there a check on invalidHashCount that set stopReason? R1F line 872 (aggregate) includes invalidHashCount in the condition. So in R1F, if a protected hash failed, invalidHashCount>0 → the aggregate at 872 set stopReason at the END, but the ACL block ran in between. So R1F also continued. Hence inherited, not a new delta. Still, the *claim* \"继承 fail-stop 语义\" is true relative to R1F (it inherits the same nuance). Hmm, the Handoff says fail-stop inherited — accurate as inheritance. But the Task/manifest says \"未知 child/path/write 立即停止\" (immediate stop for unknown child/path/write) — a hash query error isn't \"unknown path/write\". So not a violation of that clause.\n\nHowever C3 matters for the *new* phases+stopCategory feature: R1G's new code at 931-936 intends to fail-stop-and-classify at that point, but the code continues. So it's a defect in the new feature (in addition to the overwrite). I'll fold C3 into the same finding as C1/P3 (or make it P2). Let me combine: \"protectedHashes 失败分类被覆盖且不 fail-stop（继续 ACL 复查）\" → P2? The impact: on a protected-hash failure the capture continues to enumerate/read ACLs of the same host paths — extra read-only access after a stop decision, plus the specific classification is lost. It's read-only, no writes; risk small. The mandate's fail-stop concern is mainly about unknown/unauthorized access. I'll rate P3 and note it, keeping P2 for the misclassification (which is documented incorrectly).\n\nHmm, I need to be careful about how many findings and their severities so the verdict is coherent. Let me now decide the full finding list.\n\nC4 — \"754/796/823 阶段 stop 时 summary(1000)/aclStability(964) 在该次 evidence 中缺字段\". Hmm: if the `paths` phase fails (786-790), the block at 823 (`if (-not $Result.stopReason)`) is skipped → `summary` never built → the evidence lacks `summary`, `aclStability`, and `protectedHashes` stays empty dict. In R1F: the `paths`/wer blocks were inside one big `if (-not stopReason)` chain too; R1F's summary was also inside the last block (832-862). So R1F also lacked summary on early stop. Not a delta. But is it an *honesty* issue? The evidence omits `summary`; the reviewer of evidence would see the missing key. Hmm — is `summary` a declared field? It's optional in the evidence. R1F identical. Also `aclStability` only exists when the last block ran. Since R1F-inherited, I'd mention as a P3 observation or skip. Actually there's one subtlety: R1G's `New-PartialEvidence` references `$Result.summary` and `$Result.aclStability` — reading missing keys returns $null → the partial evidence includes nulls. Fine.\n\nHmm, but does the *absence* of `summary`/`aclStability` conflict with the R1G claim \"phases 未到达必须显式 NOT_REACHED\"? The phases field is always present ✓. The sub-objects are empty dicts ✓. OK.\n\nI'll mention C4 as a P3 finding? It could cause an adjudicator to misread a missing key as \"field not implemented\". Hmm. Since it's inherited and the phases field now signals reachability, impact is low. I'll fold into a P3 note rather than a separate finding. Actually — the mandate wants each finding to have severity/trigger/impact/suggestion. I could list 5-6 findings. Let me not over-inflate. I'll list the ones with real substance:\n\nFindings plan:\n\nF-001 (P2) Handoff claim vs code: stopCategory value set. Handoff line 41 lists EVIDENCE_SERIALIZATION_FAILED / EVIDENCE_SIZE_LIMIT_PARTIAL / EVIDENCE_WRITE_FAILED as stopCategory values; code assigns them to stopReason (1064/1072) or an error string (1090) and sets stopCategory='INVENTORY_OR_QUERY_STOP' for the first two (1063/1071). Impact: post-run adjudication keyed on stopCategory misclassifies an evidence-pipeline failure as an inventory/query stop; doc/code mismatch. Suggest: correct the Handoff enumeration, or (preferred) assign dedicated stopCategory values in code. Files/lines: handoff 41; capture.ps1 1063-1065, 1071-1073, 1090.\n\nF-002 (P2) Handoff AC mapping pre-empts Codex adjudication and is internally inconsistent: AC3 marked PASS while selfCheckResult=NOT_RUN and the AC3-required self-check artifact was not produced (handoff 50 vs 64; selfcheck JSON 19/33/35). AC1 is Codex-owned (freeze) yet marked \"PASS (Read-only)\" (handoff 48). Impact: 待核 claims presented as PASS could be misread as AC/gate PASS by a downstream reader; contradicts the frozen release order (manifest §Release 1-3, Task AC4). Suggest: mark AC1-AC3 as \"claim pending Codex adjudication / NOT_VERIFIED\" and only state static delivery facts.\n\nF-003 (P2) New phase-state surface cannot represent a stop that occurs in the ACL/aggregate sub-check: phases all COMPLETED (938 sets protectedHashes COMPLETED before ACL block 941-1044) while stopCategory/stopReason set at 1040-1043; also protectedHashes failure classification at 931-936 has no effect on the final stopReason (overwritten at 1042) and the script keeps doing ACL queries after the phase already failed (no return). Impact: evidence can show seven COMPLETED phases with a stop reason, and the specific PROTECTED_HASH_FAILED value is unreachable → misleading reachability reporting, weakens adjudication. Suggest: mark a phase FAILED for the ACL/aggregate stop (add aclStability phase or set protectedHashes=FAILED when the aggregate fires) and return after a protectedHash failure; don't assign unreachable values.\n\nHmm, is \"phases all COMPLETED + stop\" really reachable? So yes reachable. The paths phase already checks the same and would have stopped. So no. The realistic triggers are aclChangedCount/aclQueryErrorCount/aclUnverifiableCount. Note: aclUnverifiableCount>0 at line 1040 triggers the aggregate stop! Look at 1040: `... -or $aclUnverifiableCount -gt 0 ...`. And aclUnverifiable arises when the endpoint recheck yields empty owner/SDDL (line 956). So a single unverifiable endpoint ACL → stop with all phases COMPLETED. Reachable. Also note this means `hostAclSnapshots` classification (line 1034) says UNPROVABLE for the same condition, while stopCategory says a query stop. Fine.\n\nHmm, but hold on: is line 1040 setting stopReason a problem for the *success* semantics? It means: if any ACL endpoint recheck can't be verified, the capture is marked stopped (exit 1) even though it completed all phases. In R1F that's the same (line 872). Inherited behavior, fine.\n\nF-004 (P3) Handoff front-matter `delivery_head: 9eb14e35...` (line 6) is the implementation commit; the reviewed delivery HEAD containing the Handoff is 9ec34a88 (Task line 80; dispatch prompt line 9).
